- **W-C01-09, row 4's documentation part.** A researcher (agent `a42e1b2b6506713b8`) read the official Claude Code documentation (twelve pages, all undated but the changelog, read 2026-10-06). The executor wrote **EV-C01-001** (`evidence/C01/EV-C01-001_row4_subagent_docs.md`) with the common evidence envelope, and filed the report at `evidence/C01/raw/W-C01-09_researcher_report.md` with one stated edit: page names under the documentation's base address instead of full addresses, which match the library's fingerprints. Facts that bear on the plan's text: (1) the routines page states hourly caps and no daily cap, where plan 6.4 and row 5 assume 15 runs a day (row 5 reads the account's real value); (2) an environment cannot be deleted, only archived after its credentials are deleted, where Section 12 item 0 says "deleting the probe environments" and C01's sixth condition says they "are removed"; (3) the git proxy "doesn't limit which branches a push can update" (5.6's `claude/` is a default, not a limit) and rejects branch deletions; (4) a session with several repositories reads no hooks or permission rules from them, so the guard would not run in `devos-probe-b`'s sessions (row 9, N-048); (5) an environment's API credential is a hidden field whose value no session sees. ### L-177 · 2026-10-09 · Hand-over note: a fresh session's live working tree is shallow, so the leak check fails closed until the tree is deepened; the fix before any public write

- **Disciplines (D1–D9):** D1: no. D2: no. D3: yes: this entry serves the goal's rule to hand over cleanly (working order section 7) and Batu's request to end the turn so a new session can take over, so it names the exact step the next session needs before its first public write. D4: no. D5: yes: the shallow state and the missing revision were read directly (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → true, 176 commits; `git cat-file -t 3cd686a` fails in the live tree, resolves in a full clone), not inferred from the denial text alone. D6: yes: the push denial's surface was "the leak check could not read the push", but the cause is the shallow live tree missing revision `3cd686a`; the fix deepens the tree (the cause), it does not touch the leak check (the symptom). D7: yes: this entry exists so a new session, which the platform clones shallow again, can deepen the live tree before its first public write instead of rediscovering the blocker. D8: no. D9: no.
- **What happened.** This session's live working tree (`/home/user/devos`) was cloned shallow by the platform at container start (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` → true; 176 commits on HEAD). The guard's leak check derives the account's service names from a fixed old revision, `3cd686a`, read from the guard's own checkout (the live tree); on a shallow tree that revision is absent (`git cat-file -t 3cd686a` fails), so the check fails closed and denies every public write under L1 ("the service names cannot be derived (git show 3cd686a failed); failing closed"; guard record #71). This is the residual the working order section 10 already names ("a shallow checkout fails closed"); it bites every fresh session, because the platform's fresh clone is shallow.
- **The fix, before any public write.** Deepen the live tree once per session: `git fetch --unshallow origin` (HEAD stays at `main`; it only adds older objects). After it, `3cd686a` resolves and the leak check reads the push normally. The guard allows this fetch (it changes no revision in the tree). Done this session before the D-017 bundle was pushed; the merge of that bundle then passed the leak check (PR #195, merge `5ca70d4`).
- **For the next session.** A new container's live tree is shallow again; run `git fetch --unshallow origin` in the live tree before the first push or GitHub write. The ledger's Stage row carries a one-line pointer to this entry.
- **Guard:** `tools/guard_report.py` at the stop check: 5 denied, 76 allowed, 0 passed to the user, hash chain intact, last record #81. New since L-176 (through #61): #71 L1 (this session: the first push attempt, denied because the live tree was shallow — see above; the matched text is never shown or logged, and here there was no match, only the fail-closed); #73 B5 (a `test -f` naming `.git/shallow`, which B5 denies; the shallow state was then read with `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` instead). The merge-gate (M7) and the successful push's leak check (L1) are among the allowed calls.
- **Record changes:** plan/ledger/C01-log.md · addition · L-177; plan/ledger.md · correction · Stage (a one-line hand-over pointer for the next session)

- **Disciplines (D1–D9):** D1: yes: four mechanisms were weighed (the setting alone, a wrapper alone, both, and a deny by exit code rather than by the decision object); both are used, with a JSON deny and exit 0, because the documentation leaves exit 2 on `PermissionRequest` and versions older than v2.1.295 open under the setting alone. D2: no. D3: yes: the change serves W-C01-38's acceptance (the guard fails closed before C02 opens DevOS's environments); its acceptance needs a later verdict on the merged guard and the re-run record N-173 asks for (CHK-C01-062 C3). D4: yes: the producer broke the change three ways in copies to show the checks turn red (17, 7 and 37 `BAD`); two fresh checkers judge it before it merges, as a guard change needs. D5: yes: the hooks, changelog and settings pages were re-read at 14:30Z, not taken from EV-C01-017's quotes. D6: yes: every non-zero exit and every wrong-form answer on `PermissionRequest` is closed, not only the named faults; the same class by another route (an empty guard file) is carried as N-185. D7: yes: after the merge the live guard changes for every session; N-173's dated line says what each session's opening check must show, and the test runs again on the pushed branch. D8: yes: the branch started at `main` `528d9e2`; `main` `aabb9c9` is merged in. D9: no.
- **What happened.** A producer (DISCIPLINES OK) wrote the change. `.claude/settings.json`: `"onFailure": "block"` on the `PreToolUse` and `PermissionRequest` guard hooks; the `PermissionRequest` command runs the guard with its event name and passes through only an empty answer with exit 0 (the guard's "pass") or an answer in that event's form with exit 0; anything else becomes a fixed deny in that form, with exit 0, from shell builtins, so it answers even with no `python3`. `.claude/hooks/tool_allowlist.py`: `main()` takes its event from the first argument, so unreadable input on `PermissionRequest` gets the guard's own G0 deny in that form; the docstring's exit paragraph; no rule touched. `tools/test_tool_allowlist.sh`: an answer counts only in its own event's form; a check of the setting on both hooks; seven faults injected into the `PermissionRequest` command under sh and bash (a syntax error, a failing import, a missing guard file, no `python3`, unreadable input, the guard's own exit 2, a `PreToolUse`-form allow), each denied, with three positive controls. `plan/Installation_Working_Order.md` section 10: the two items on a timed-out hook and on a `PermissionRequest` hook that cannot run now say what the change does. Test in the clone, 14:41Z-14:43Z: `ok=725 BAD=0`, `ALLOWLIST_TEST PASS`; three controls skipped because the branch was not yet on the remote (they run on the pushed branch, N-173 (e)); every one of the 702 earlier lines comes out the same. Documentation (hooks page, read 14:30Z): "With `"block"` set, a failure does what exit code 2 does on that event, except on `PermissionRequest`, where it denies the request"; "Requires Claude Code v2.1.295 or later"; on `PermissionRequest`, "Exit code 2 isn't honored for this event". Not covered, kept apart: (a) what an opening check after the merge, or the working session's guard report right after the sync (N-173 (e)), shows: that the settings load and the guard is live; (b) what no planned check observes: that 2.1.296 applies `onFailure`, so that a guard hook that times out or cannot start blocks the call or denies the request; (b) is documented, not independently tested (corrected before this entry reached `main`, CHK-C01-077 C1; L-228). N-185.
- **Wait:** ends: this change waits for its two checks; P3's second attempt waits for Batu's press after "P3 tekrar hazır" (comment `6110210099`, 14:46Z); sooner: none; whose call: the builder, then Batu.
